Result: Morphology manifolds support diagnostic, molecular, and survival predictions and localize informative regions; the inferred tumor-state axes are spatially coherent, associate with clinicopathological variables and survival, and are not mere recapitulations of stage/diagnosis; integrated omics reveal programs in chromatin regulation, copy-number driven variation, RTK signaling, cell adhesion, ECM remodeling, and metabolic adaptation; perturbation highlights features coupled to state organization.

Conclusion: Uterine histopathology contains recoverable progression-associated tumor-state information; SpaTIE offers a framework to connect spatial morphology with multi-omics-defined tumor states, enabling discovery of progression-related programs and coherent spatial organization across large cohorts.

Abstract: Tumor progression is accompanied by changes in architecture, morphology and microenvironmental organization, yet progression-associated heterogeneity is usually compressed into static diagnostic categories in histopathology. Here we present SpaTIE, a uterus-specific computational pathology framework that learns morphology-aware representations and organizes spatial histopathological heterogeneity into progression-associated tumor states. SpaTIE was developed using 10,426 uterine hematoxylin and eosin whole-slide images and evaluated in TCGA-UCEC and TCGA-UCS cohorts. The learned representations formed morphology manifolds, supported diagnostic, molecular and survival-related prediction tasks, and localized attention to informative tumor regions. Beyond supervised prediction, SpaTIE inferred tumor-state axes from cross-sectional morphology without temporal or molecular supervision. These morphology-derived states were spatially coherent and showed associations with clinicopathological variables and survival outcomes, while not simply recapitulating staging or diagnostic labels. Integrative multi-omics analyses linked the inferred states to DNA methylation, somatic copy-number variation, mutation, RNA-seq and RPPA profiles, highlighting molecular programs related to chromatin regulation, copy-number-associated structural variation, receptor tyrosine kinase signaling, cell adhesion, extracellular-matrix remodeling and metabolic adaptation. Progression-guided virtual perturbation further prioritized molecular features coupled to the morphology-derived state organization. Together, these findings suggest that uterine histopathology contains recoverable progression-associated tumor-state information and establish SpaTIE as a framework for connecting spatial morphology with multi-omics-informed tumor-state discovery.

</details>


### [25] [Primitive-Driven Compositional Forensic Visual Prompting for Open-World Face Anti-Spoofing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17351)
*Fangling Jiang,Qi Li,Bing Liu,Weining Wang,Quilin Huang,Zhenan Sun,Ming-Hsuan Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes a visual-only, compositional prompt learning framework for open-world face anti-spoofing that uses patch-aware refinement of micro-forensic primitives and class-specific routing prompts, yielding state-of-the-art generalization to unseen attacks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Open-world spoofing presents covariate and semantic shifts between source/target domains; existing prompt-based methods rely on high-level semantics and struggle with evolving, fine-grained spatial forensic cues of unseen attacks.

Method: Freeze ViT backbone; use patch-aware attention to refine a shared set of micro-forensic primitives into localized evidence units derived from image patches. Class-specific global contextual prompts provide routing weights to compose primitives into compositional visual prompts for real/spoof discrimination. Primitives lack predefined semantics; specialization emerges through joint optimization across categories.

Result: Extensive experiments across nine open-world protocols show state-of-the-art performance, strong cross-domain generalization, and robust adaptation to unseen attacks.

Conclusion: A purely visual, compositional prompt framework effectively captures unseen spoofing cues by composing localized primitives guided by global prompts, enabling robust open-world face anti-spoofing.

Abstract: Open-world face anti-spoofing must address both covariate and semantic shifts: source and target domains differ in imaging conditions, while target domains contain diverse attack types absent from training. Existing prompt-based approaches often express spoofing through category semantics or language guidance, which is effective for modeling high-level concepts but is less suited to explicitly capturing the evolving fine-grained and spatially heterogeneous forensic evidence of unseen attacks. Motivated by the hypothesis that many unseen attacks can be characterized by new combinations of recurring visual cues, we propose a compositional forensic visual prompt learning framework that operates entirely in the visual feature space.Built on a frozen ViT-based vision foundation model, the framework employs patch-aware attention to refine a shared set of learnable micro-forensic primitives into localized forensic evidence units derived from image patches. Class-specific global contextual prompts then provide input-dependent routing weights that adaptively select and compose these primitives into compositional forensic visual prompts for real/spoof discrimination. The primitives are not assigned predefined semantic meanings; instead, their specialization and reuse emerge from shared parameterization and joint optimization across categories.Extensive experiments on nine open-world protocols demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, strong cross-domain generalization, and robust adaptation to unseen attacks.

</details>


### [26] [Continuity-Driven Representation Learning for Industrial Defect Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17362)
*Minjong Kim,Hyun Jun Kim,Jeongrae Kim,Heeseung Shin,Changwon Lim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Continuity-driven representation regularization for industrial defect detection using dense normal-region supervision; introduces Multi-Continuity Loss and Differencing Loss; yields improvements across multiple detectors and datasets, especially with limited data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Industrial defect inspection features dominant normal regions and repetitive patterns. Conventional detectors rely on sparse bounding-box supervision, leading to weak normal-region representations. A dense, continuity-based prior on normal regions can stabilize features while preserving defect-induced discontinuities.

Method: Two detector-agnostic objectives: (1) Multi-Continuity Loss combining 1D patch-sequence prediction and 2D masked spatial prediction; (2) Differencing Loss regularizing first-order feature variation and second-order curvature between neighboring patch embeddings. Region weighting derived from box annotations stabilizes normal regions while preserving discontinuities. Compatible with YOLO-family, MambaYOLO, DETR.

Result: Experiments on two real-world industrial datasets, MEA, and NEU-DET across six detectors show consistent improvements over baselines. Full-data gains in mAP@0.5:0.95: up to 3.49 pp (Industrial Metal), 5.38 pp (MEA), 5.03 pp (NEU-DET). Limited-data gains (25% data): Differencing Loss yields up to 21.07 pp in mAP@0.5 and 8.23 pp in mAP@0.5:0.95 on NEU-DET.

Conclusion: Continuity-driven regularization provides an effective prior for industrial defect detection, particularly beneficial when annotated data are scarce.

Abstract: Industrial defect detection differs from natural-image object detection because inspection images are captured under controlled conditions and contain large normal-dominant regions with repetitive structures. Defects therefore appear as localized disruptions of otherwise predictable patterns, while conventional detectors rely mainly on sparse bounding-box supervision, resulting in weakly constrained normal-region representations. We propose a continuity-driven representation regularization framework that exploits normal-dominant regions as dense auxiliary supervision. The framework introduces two detector-agnostic objectives: Multi-Continuity Loss, which combines 1D patch-sequence prediction and 2D masked spatial prediction, and Differencing Loss, which regularizes first-order feature variation and second-order curvature between neighboring patch embeddings. Both objectives are applied with box-derived region weighting to stabilize normal-region representations while preserving defect-related discontinuities.
  Experiments on two real-world industrial datasets and the public NEU-DET benchmark, using six detector architectures including YOLO-family models, MambaYOLO, and DETR, demonstrate consistent improvements over native detector baselines. In the full-data setting, the proposed regularizers improve average mAP@0.5:0.95 by up to 3.49 percentage points on Industrial Metal, 5.38 percentage points on MEA, and 5.03 percentage points on NEU-DET. Under limited-data conditions, the gains become more pronounced, with Differencing Loss achieving improvements of up to 21.07 percentage points in mAP@0.5 and 8.23 percentage points in mAP@0.5:0.95 on NEU-DET using only 25% of the training data. These results suggest that continuity-driven regularization provides an effective prior for improving industrial defect detection, particularly when annotated data are scarce.

</details>


### [27] [GeoWeaver: Accurate Long-Sequence 3D Reconstruction via Hierarchical Geometric Assembly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17389)
*Tinghao Jiang,Sheng Tang,Shengzhe Wei,Juntong Fang,Weiqi Zhang,Junsheng Zhou,Zesong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoWeaver introduces a unified long-sequence 3D reconstruction framework that couples a Geometric Prior Model (GPM) with Test-Time Adaptation (TTA). It maintains local geometric accuracy while enforcing global consistency across chunks via Sim(3 alignment, dense correspondences, and a robust CDF-style objective, improving camera pose, scale, depth, and calibration across long RGB video sequences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long RGB sequences pose memory challenges for end-to-end models and lead to drift when chunking. Feed-forward approaches are fast but cannot jointly optimize over long horizons; chunk-wise methods accumulate scale drift, pose errors, and misalignment. A unified framework that leverages geometric priors and test-time refinement can enforce global consistency without prohibitive memory costs.

Method: GPM predicts chunk-wise depth, confidence, and camera parameters as adjustable priors. TTA performs sequential initialization, global chunk-level Sim(3 alignment), and coarse-to-fine refinement of poses, depth, and intrinsics. Dense correspondences supply cross-chunk constraints. A robust, CDF-style objective combines weighted 2D reprojection and 3D consistency residuals to preserve local geometry while correcting pose, scale, depth, and calibration drift. The approach is evaluated across long sequences and across different GPMs to demonstrate generality.

Result: Experiments on diverse long-sequence benchmarks show improved camera accuracy, global consistency, and higher-quality point clouds. Ablations confirm the contribution of each adaptation stage. The same TTA procedure improves trajectory estimates when applied to different GPMs, demonstrating that GeoWeaver is not tied to a single geometric prior.

Conclusion: GeoWeaver provides a unified, generalizable mechanism to achieve both accurate local geometry and global trajectory consistency in long-sequence 3D reconstruction. By integrating a Geometric Prior Model with Test-Time Adaptation and dense constraints, it mitigates scale drift, pose drift, and calibration errors across chunks, yielding robust improvements in camera parameters and point-cloud quality.

Abstract: Long-sequence 3D reconstruction from RGB videos requires both accurate local geometry and globally consistent camera motion. Feed-forward models provide strong depth and pose predictions, but their memory cost prevents joint inference over long sequences. Chunk-wise processing improves scalability, yet independently predicted chunks often exhibit scale drift, pose errors, and point-cloud misalignment. We present GeoWeaver, a unified framework comprising a Geometric Prior Model (GPM) and Test-Time Adaptation (TTA). The GPM predicts chunk-wise depth, confidence, and camera parameters as adjustable geometric priors. TTA then performs sequential initialization, global chunk-level Sim(3) alignment, and coarse-to-fine refinement of camera poses, affine depth corrections, and intrinsics. Dense correspondences provide adjacent, cross-chunk, and long-range constraints, while a robust CDF-style objective jointly optimizes weighted 2D reprojection and 3D consistency residuals. This design preserves local geometric accuracy while correcting accumulated pose, scale, depth, and calibration errors. Experiments across diverse long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate improved camera accuracy, global consistency, and point-cloud quality. Ablations verify the contribution of each adaptation stage, and applying the same TTA procedure to different geometric prior models consistently improves their trajectory estimates, demonstrating that GeoWeaver is not tied to a specific GPM.

</details>


### [28] [Noisy group neurons with synchronous resetting for high-performance spiking neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17394)
*Yajie Zhai,Yanmei Kang,Meng Li,Zigang Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A noisy group neuron (NGN) framework for deep spiking neural networks that uses population-level synchronous resetting and neural stochasticity, paired with mean-field backpropagation, to mitigate spatiotemporal information loss and gradient misalignment, achieving strong results on neuromorphic and standard datasets (e.g., 87.35% on CIFAR10-DVS in 10 steps).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address key training challenges in deep SNNs, notably spatiotemporal information loss and gradient mismatching, to enable high-performance neuromorphic computing.

Method: Introduce the NGN model with population-level synchronous resetting and neural stochasticity; develop NGN training framework using backpropagation based on mean-field dynamics; validate theoretically and empirically across standard and neuromorphic datasets.

Result: The NGN framework demonstrates competitive performance across CIFAR-10/100, Tiny-ImageNet, DVS-Gesture, N-Caltech101, and CIFAR10-DVS, including 87.35% accuracy on CIFAR10-DVS within 10 inference steps, supported by theoretical analysis.

Conclusion: NGN offers a practical and effective approach for high-performance neuromorphic computing, combining biologically inspired stochasticity and synchronized resets with principled mean-field backpropagation.

Abstract: Spiking neural networks (SNNs), characterized by bio-inspired neuronal dynamics and event-driven communication, have attained significant progress in recent years. Nevertheless, training deep SNNs remains challenging due to spatiotemporal information loss and gradient mismatching. To simultaneously address these issues, we propose a noisy group neuron (NGN) model, which incorporates population-level synchronous resetting and neural stochasticity as fundamental computational mechanisms. We then develop the NGN method as a framework that combines the NGN model with backpropagation learning based on mean-field dynamics. We demonstrate the advantages of the NGN method through theoretical analysis and experimental validation on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, DVS-Gesture, N-Caltech101, and CIFAR10-DVS. The proposed approach achieves an accuracy of 87.35% on CIFAR10-DVS within 10 inference time steps. These results support NGN as a practical approach to high-performance neuromorphic computing.

</details>


### [29] [To Remove or Not to Remove Clouds: A Comparative Analysis and Fusion of Raw SAR and Synthetic NDWI for Overcast Water Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17398)
*Saleh Sakib Ahmed,Sara Nowreen,M. Sohel Rahman*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Synthetic NDWI translation improves flood water mapping over raw SAR under cloudy conditions; a Combined Framework that fuses raw SAR with synthetic NDWI further enhances segmentation performance compared to single-source approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cloud cover prevents optical imagery during floods; determine whether raw SAR or translated synthetic NDWI yields better water-body segmentation, and whether a hybrid approach leveraging both sources can outperform either approach alone.

Method: 1) Compare segmentation using raw SAR vs synthetic NDWI under complete overcast. 2) Demonstrate that translation to synthetic NDWI acts as a denoising filter for radar noise, yielding better performance. 3) Propose and implement a Combined Framework that fuses raw SAR and synthetic NDWI into a single model. 4) Evaluate on water-body segmentation tasks under cloudy conditions, comparing against standalone methods.

Result: Synthetic NDWI yields better segmentation performance than raw SAR due to denoising and enhanced contrast; the Combined Framework that merges raw SAR and synthetic NDWI consistently outperforms both single-source approaches.

Conclusion: Integrating raw SAR with synthetic NDWI provides the best performance for flood-water mapping under overcast conditions, leveraging the sharp edges of SAR and the high-contrast, denoised representation of synthetic NDWI.

Abstract: Persistent clouds blind optical satellites during floods. While Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) penetrates clouds, its raw data is noisy and lacks clear contrast. To mitigate this, recent studies utilize deep learning models to translate SAR into cloud-free synthetic optical imagery for downstream tasks like water body segmentation. However, because raw SAR is the original source for both of these operations, a critical methodological dilemma arises: during complete overcast should segmentation models process the raw SAR directly, or rely on a translated synthetic Normalized Difference Water Index (NDWI) proxy? This study resolves the debate by demonstrating that synthetic NDWI yields better results, as the translation process acts as a powerful filter against radar noise. This raises a natural second question: what if we utilize both? Building on our findings, we introduce a Combined Framework that integrates both raw SAR and synthetic NDWI into a unified model. By fusing the sharp physical boundaries of raw SAR with the high contrast of synthetic NDWI, this hybrid approach consistently outperforms all standalone methods.

</details>


### [30] [MoE-ViE: Mixture of Experts Vision Encoder for Efficient Image and Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17402)
*Bonan Zhang,Shiyu Dong,Quan Hung Tran,Katharina Gschwind,Shuqi Yang,Sijia Chen,Adel Ahmadyan,Seungwhan Moon,Lu Zhang,Ahmed Kirmani,Babak Damavandi,Anuj Kumar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Fine-grained Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) scaling for vision encoders (MoE-ViE) yields substantial gains over dense and standard MoE baselines, with latency-aware design and auxiliary-balanced variants; frame-level distillation extends to video; largest model matches SOTA zero-shot at 1.7x smaller size and 76% of latency, surpassing many encoders when paired with an LLM; code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficiently scaling vision encoders without prohibitive compute and latency by leveraging MoE architectures, addressing the underexplored MoE design space for CLIP-style encoders at SOTA performance.

Method: Systematic exploration of MoE topologies for vision encoders; development of an auxiliary-loss-free balancing variant to improve expert utilization; design of a specialized MoE kernel to mitigate inference latency; frame-level distillation with a freezing mechanism to extend video capabilities; pretrained a family of MoE-ViE encoders across sizes; alignment with LLMs to boost image and video performance; release of code.

Result: Across sizes, MoE-ViE encoders consistently outperform dense counterparts. The largest model matches SOTA zero-shot performance 1.7x smaller in size while using only 76% of the latency. When combined with an LLM, MoE-ViE surpasses all compared encoders on image and video benchmarks, even those with up to 5x more activated parameters.

Conclusion: Fine-grained MoE topologies with an auxiliary-balanced design and latency-aware kernels enable efficient scaling of vision encoders. Frame-level distillation extends capabilities to video while preserving image knowledge; the approach yields strong image/video performance with reduced latency, and code release facilitates adoption.

Abstract: Vision encoders are a critical component of vision-language models, and scaling their capacity effectively improves performance. However, dense scaling increases compute cost and inference latency. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures offer a compelling alternative, having enabled efficient scaling in LLMs, yet the MoE design space for CLIP-style vision encoders remains underexplored at State-of-the-Art (SOTA) levels. In this work, we systematically study MoE designs for vision encoder scaling and find that fine-grained MoE topologies yield substantial gains over both dense and standard MoE counterparts. We further propose an auxiliary-loss-free balancing variant for better expert utilization, and design a specialized MoE kernel to mitigate inference latency overhead. To enhance video capabilities while preserving image knowledge, we introduce frame-level distillation paired with a novel freezing mechanism. We pretrain a series of Mixture-of-Experts Vision Encoders (MoE-ViE) across a range of sizes, all consistently outperforming their dense counterparts. Our largest model matches the zero-shot performance of a SOTA encoder 1.7x its size at 76% of its latency. When aligned with an LLM, MoE-ViE surpasses all compared encoders on image and video benchmarks, including those with up to 5x more activated parameters. Code is available at https://github.com/facebookresearch/moe_vie.

</details>


### [31] [REChart: Reasoning-Efficient Chart Editing with Large Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17414)
*Yuanbang Liu,Chenxi Ruan,Yihan Hou,Qiong Luo,Wei Zeng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: REChart introduces a two-stage supervision framework to improve chart editing with large language models by providing process-level intermediate reasoning supervision and reinforcement learning rewards (fidelity and efficiency). It mitigates overthinking, reduces reasoning tokens, and achieves state-of-the-art open-source performance on ChartEdit/ChartMIMIC benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Chart editing requires seamless integration of visual reasoning, instruction following, and executable code synthesis. Prior work shows an inverted-U relationship between reasoning length and performance, indicating that excessive reasoning harms outcomes. There is a need for process-level supervision to improve both fidelity and efficiency in multimodal chart-editing tasks.

Method: Synthesize 200k high-quality reasoning trajectories via a role-specialized Reason-Score-Refine workflow to supervise fine-tuning. Train with supervised fine-tuning on the trajectories. Then apply reinforcement learning with two rewards: (1) fidelity reward assessing code correctness, visual fidelity, and structural consistency, and (2) efficiency reward that assigns a random thinking budget, truncates subsequent reasoning, and credits the final reasoning segment according to its contribution. Evaluate on ChartEdit and ChartMIMIC benchmarks with comparisons to open-source models of similar scale.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art chart-editing performance among open-source models of comparable scale on ChartEdit/ChartMIMIC, while mitigating overthinking and reducing average reasoning token usage by 79.0% under a maximum thinking budget of 16,384 tokens.

Conclusion: Process-level supervision and reinforcement learning rewards effectively improve editing fidelity and reasoning efficiency for multimodal chart editing, addressing overthinking and enabling more economical use of reasoning tokens in large models.

Abstract: Chart editing requires inferring and modifying visualization code from a reference chart image based on an editing instruction, challenging fine-grained visual reasoning, instruction following, and executable code synthesis capabilities of MLLMs. Large reasoning models (LRMs) with extended Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning are suitable for tackling such complex multimodal tasks. However, our preliminary study reveals an ``inverted-U'' relationship between reasoning length and chart-editing performance: Excessive reasoning often leads to ``overthinking,'' where models drift toward hallucinated visual details or get stuck in redundant reasoning loops. To address the gap, we introduce REChart, a two-stage training framework that provides process-level supervision over intermediate reasoning steps, improving both editing fidelity and reasoning efficiency. First, we synthesize 200k high-quality reasoning trajectories for supervised fine-tuning from a large image-instruction-code pool, using a role-specialized agentic Reason-Score-Refine workflow that iteratively refine the chart code toward higher quality. Second, we optimize the model via reinforcement learning with two complementary rewards: a \emph{fidelity} reward evaluating code correctness, visual fidelity, and structural consistency, and an \emph{efficiency} reward that assigns each rollout a random thinking budget, truncates the reasoning process, and credits the final reasoning segment according to its contribution to the output. On the ChartEdit and ChartMIMIC benchmarks, our model achieves state-of-the-art chart-editing performance among open-source models of comparable scale, while mitigating overthinking and reducing average reasoning token usage by 79.0\% under a maximum thinking budget of 16,384 tokens compared with the base model.

</details>


### [32] [Spectral Gradient Orthogonalization Improves Differentially Private Training at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17415)
*Sabari Shanmugam,Nick Barnes,Kerry Taylor*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Spectral gradient orthogonalization via polar decomposition improves DP training in high-SNR, large-batch regimes, with substantial accuracy gains and reduced variance; utility exhibits a phase transition depending on per-direction spectral SNR and gradient spectral gap; benefits are limited to moderate-to-high-SNR settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: DP-SGD degrades utility by adding isotropic noise to a high-dimensional, low-rank gradient structure typical of vision models. A post-processing step that recovers signal directions could boost utility without extra privacy cost.

Method: Apply spectral gradient orthogonalization using polar decomposition as a post-processing post-forward step to noisy clipped gradients. Analyze a phase transition governed by per-direction SNR and gradient spectral gap. Optionally combine with temporal denoising. Evaluate on large-batch, high-capacity models and fine-tuning scenarios.

Result: Empirical gains include +20.9% top-1 accuracy over DP-SGD on WRN-28-10 (B=4096) and +14.9% on ResNet-18; inter-run variance reduced by 2–3x. In fine-tuning, matches DP-Adam stability with lower memory. Temporal denoising plus spectral method yields 50.3% on CIFAR-10 (epsilon=4). Benefits scale with model capacity and batch size; small-batch/low-SNR settings prefer DP-SGD or temporal denoising.

Conclusion: Spectral orthogonalization is effective in moderate-to-high-SNR regimes with large batches and higher-capacity models. It provides notable accuracy gains and variance reduction while maintaining privacy, but provides little or even harm in low-SNR or small-batch settings; combining with temporal denoising can yield the best results in suitable configurations.

Abstract: Differentially private training adds isotropic Gaussian noise to clipped gradients, corrupting every singular direction equally. In vision models, where spatial correlation concentrates gradient energy into a low-rank subspace, most of this noise falls in directions that carry little signal. Spectral gradient orthogonalization via polar decomposition is introduced as a post-processing step that recovers directional signal from the noisy gradient's low-rank structure at zero additional privacy cost. A phase transition governs the utility of this approach: orthogonalization improves accuracy only when the per-direction spectral signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) suffices for singular vector recovery; in low-SNR regimes, the directional bias of the gradient is replaced by a nearly random orthogonal update, and the transformation is harmful. The recovery threshold is determined by the spectral gap of the gradient and is surpassed at large batch sizes. Empirically, the benefit scales with model capacity: spectral orthogonalization achieves a +20.9% improvement over DP-SGD on WRN-28-10 (B = 4096) and +14.9% on ResNet-18, while reducing inter-run variance by a factor of two to three. In the fine-tuning regime, spectral orthogonalization matches the stability of DP-Adam while maintaining a first-order memory footprint. Combining spectral with temporal denoising yields 50.3% on CIFAR-10 (epsilon = 4), the highest accuracy in any tested configuration. These gains are specific to moderate-to-high-SNR regimes such as large-batch training of higher-capacity models. Small-batch or low-SNR settings are better served by DP-SGD or temporal denoising.

</details>


### [33] [SPVC: Structured and Panoptic Video Fixing for Cross-Dataset Driving Scene Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17420)
*Gen Li,Shu Han,Yun Xi Qiao,Hua Chen,Xuyang Dai,Bohan Li,Hao Zhao,Chaojian Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SPVC presents a cross-dataset, structured, panoptic video fixer for driving-scene rendering with 3D Gaussian splatting, driven by explicit spatial constraints and a two-stage controllable diffusion model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rendered driving scenes degrade under extrapolated ego trajectories and edits; existing fixers are typically specialized to single settings. A unified, cross-dataset approach leveraging structure, panoptic consistency, and temporal cues is desirable.

Method: Build paired degraded-clean data by simulating under-constrained 3D Gaussian splatting and foreground vehicle insertions. Train a two-stage controllable video diffusion model that first fixes video-level appearance, then refines scene layout using structured controls (camera pose, 3D bounding boxes, HD maps).

Result: Aims to reduce blur, flicker, and foreground-background misalignment across sequences and datasets, enabling a shared network to generalize across datasets and handle both background and foreground artifacts with temporal coherence.

Conclusion: Four design principles—Structured, Panoptic, Video, and Cross-dataset fixing—are operationalized via a two-stage diffusion framework to robustly repair driving scene renderings in cross-dataset settings.

Abstract: Driving scene reconstruction and rendering, especially with 3D Gaussian Splatting, has become an important component of autonomous driving simulation. However, rendered views often degrade under extrapolated ego trajectories and scene edits, producing blurry structures, temporal flicker, and foreground-background misalignment. Existing refinement methods are commonly designed for a specific setting, such as image-level novel-view repair or object-editing correction. In this paper, we introduce SPVC, a structured and panoptic video fixing framework for cross-dataset driving scene rendering. The name summarizes four design principles. (1) Structured fixing denotes the use of explicit spatial conditions, including camera pose, 3D bounding boxes, and HD maps, to guide the repair process and reduce uncontrolled hallucination. (2) Panoptic fixing refers to correcting both background rendering artifacts, such as distorted roads, buildings, and lanes, and foreground vehicle artifacts introduced by scene editing, such as inconsistent object appearance. (3) Video fixing means that the model operates on driving sequences rather than isolated frames, allowing temporal cues to be used during artifact correction. (4) Cross-dataset fixing means that a single shared network is trained and applied across multiple driving datasets, reducing the need for dataset-specific or scene-specific fixers. Concretely, we construct paired degraded-clean training data by simulating under-constrained 3DGS rendering and foreground vehicle insertion artifacts, and train a two-stage controllable video diffusion model that first addresses video-level appearance and then refines scene layout with structured controls.

</details>


### [34] [TEAMS: Text-prompted spatiotEmporal dual-heAd Mamba Snake](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17421)
*Ruicheng Zhang,Jianhui Lei,Kaiwen Shen,Haowei Guo,Jun Zhou,Bin Chen,Mengtang Li,Shen Zhao,Shuo Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TEAMS introduces a vision-language, snake-based segmentation framework with three innovations—spatiotemporal evolution of the contour (SSES), morphology-aware contour modulation in a dual-head Mamba ('CMAM'), and text-prompted collaboration to refine detections (TCDHS). It targets complex organ morphology, fine-grained detail capture, and error correction in deep snakes, achieving notable gains across five datasets (e.g., +6.9% mDice, +9.1% mBF on spinal data).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address key limitations of existing deep snake segmentation: handling complex morphological variations, preserving fine-grained organ details, and correcting base detection errors; exploit temporal evolution and textual guidance to improve robustness and accuracy in medical image segmentation.

Method: 1) Spatiotemporal Snake Evolution Strategy (SSES): bidirectional spatial dependencies along the contour and temporal dynamics across evolution steps within a state-space model. 2) Contour Morphology-Aware Mamba (CMAM): quantifies local contour morphologies to modulate the structured attention mask in the Mamba2 SSD dual form, prioritizing input sequence elements for fine-grained delineation. 3) Text-prompted Collaborative Dual-Head Snake (TCDHS): leverages textual prompts to guide segmentation and transfers evolved contour information to the base detection head, enabling collaborative refinement.

Result: Comprehensive evaluation on five datasets spanning various organs and imaging modalities shows TEAMS outperforms semantic and existing deep snake methods, with notable gains such as ~6.9% relative mDice and ~9.1% mBF on a spinal dataset, indicating improved delineation and reduced misdetections.

Conclusion: TEAMS demonstrates strong potential as a robust medical image segmentation tool across diverse modalities, addressing morphological variation, fine-grained morphology, and erroneous detections; the approach offers a cohesive integration of spatiotemporal contour evolution, morphology-aware attention, and text-guided collaboration that can be extended to other domains.

Abstract: Deep snake is a promising family of instance segmentation methods that accurately predicts object-level contours, thereby overcoming common pixel-level misclassification issues such as mask cavities and jagged edges in semantic segmentation approaches. However, existing deep snake methods face challenges in handling complex morphological variations, accurately capturing fine-grained organ details, and correcting base detection errors. To mitigate these limitations, we propose a cohesive Text-prompted spatiotEmporal dual-heAd Mamba Snake (TEAMS), a novel vision-language Mamba snake framework with three key innovations: (1) A Spatiotemporal Snake Evolution Strategy (SSES) is introduced to tackle complex morphological variations by capturing bidirectional spatial dependencies along the snake contour and temporal dynamics across evolution steps in a state space model. (2) A Contour Morphology-Aware Mamba (CMAM) is proposed to quantify local contour morphologies to modulate the structured attention mask in the Mamba2 SSD dual form, which extends Mamba's capability to perceive the relative importance of its input sequence elements for better delineation of fine-grained organ details. (3) A Text-prompted Collaborative Dual-Head Snake (TCDHS) is designed to incorporate cues from textual prompts and transfer the evolved contour information to the base detection head, which enhances the deep snake workflow and mitigates wrong detections. Comprehensive evaluations on five datasets covering different organs and imaging modalities demonstrate that TEAMS outperforms existing semantic and deep snake segmentation methods (e.g., relative mDice/mBF improvements of 6.9%/9.1% in a spinal dataset), underscoring its potential as a reliable tool across diverse medical image segmentation scenarios.

</details>


### [35] [TF-CADE: Foreground-Concentrated Text-Video Alignment for Zero-Shot Temporal Action Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17422)
*Yearang Lee,Ho-Joong Kim,Seong-Whan Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TF-CADE introduces Action Concentrate Aggregation (ACA) and Certainty-based Confidence Re-weighting (CCR) to align text with action-relevant foreground in zero-shot temporal action detection, achieving state-of-the-art in-distribution results and strong cross-dataset generalization to unseen actions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current ZSTAD methods rely on text-video alignment but struggle to capture semantic distinctions between action classes, leading to text-irrelevant predictions. There is a need for foreground-focused alignment to enhance semantic consistency and inter-class discriminability.

Method: Proposes Text-Foreground Concentrated Alignment for zero-shot temporal action DEtector (TF-CADE). Key components: (1) Action Concentrate Aggregation (ACA) to compute action concentrate scores and form a foreground-weighted video embedding by aggregating temporally informative segments; (2) Certainty-based Confidence Re-weighting (CCR) to refine per-snippet confidences using foreground-aware similarity, suppressing irrelevant action classes during inference.

Result: Extensive evaluations show TF-CADE achieves state-of-the-art performance under in-distribution settings and demonstrates strong cross-dataset generalization to unseen action classes.

Conclusion: Foreground-concentrated alignment via ACA and CCR enhances semantic consistency and inter-class discriminability in ZSTAD, yielding robust performance and better cross-dataset generalization.

Abstract: Zero-Shot Temporal Action Detection (ZSTAD) aims to lo- calize and recognize action instances from unseen action categories in untrimmed videos. Although existing meth- ods have shown effectiveness by advancing architectural text-video alignment, they still struggle with capturing se- mantic distinctions between action classes, resulting in text- irrelevant predictions. To address this issue, we propose a Text-Foreground Concentrated Alignment for zero-shot temporal action DEtector (TF-CADE) that explicitly aligns textual information with action-relevant foreground regions. Specifically, we introduce Action Concentrate Aggregation (ACA), which extracts action concentrate scores to aggregate temporally informative video segments into a foreground- weighted video embedding. This foreground concentrated alignment enhances the semantic consistency between text and video features and improves inter-class discriminabil- ity. In addition, a Certainty-based Confidence Re-weighting (CCR) strategy refines per-snippet confidence scores by lever- aging foreground-aware similarity, effectively suppressing irrelevant action classes during inference. Extensive evalua- tions show that our TF-CADE not only achieves state-of-the- art performance under in-distribution settings but also excels in cross-dataset generalization to unseen action classes.

</details>


### [36] [GSToken: Geometry-Structured Gaussian Tokens for Compact 3D Medical Image Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17425)
*Xiaoduo Li,Quan Gu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Gaussian tokens for 3D multi-modal brain tumor segmentation, embedding explicit geometric attributes (center, anisotropic scale, orientation) into tokens; pairs with a frozen-token utility protocol to assess information retention under fixed capacity, showing GSToken outperforms capacity-matched baselines across tumor regions and metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing tokenization methods for 3D medical images compress volumetric data into sequences but discard shape information; evaluating tokenizer quality is confounded by downstream decoder capacity; there is a need for a unified capacity contract and explicit geometric encoding to improve information density in 3D representations.

Method: Propose Gaussian tokens that encode semantic features plus learned 3D center, anisotropic scale, and orientation. Use a frozen-token utility evaluation: freeze the trained tokenizer, serialize outputs into a fixed-capacity representation, and evaluate with a lightweight Transformer probe under matched conditions. Compare GSToken to capacity-matched adaptive baselines with multi-seed paired statistical testing.

Result: GSToken consistently and substantially outperforms capacity-matched baselines when probed under the frozen-token protocol, with uniform improvements across all tumor sub-regions, surface, and distance metrics.

Conclusion: Explicitly encoding spatial geometry in tokens increases information density of volumetric representations and offers a new design principle for compact 3D medical image representation and improved downstream reading.

Abstract: Effective segmentation of multi-modal MRI is central to improving neural network accuracy in brain tumor recognition. Existing methods typically compress 3D volumes into token sequences via fixed patch encoding or learned attention pooling (e.g., TokenLearner). However, these compression schemes discard explicit spatial shape information; the resulting tokens convey no notion of lesion morphology or spatial extent. Meanwhile, end-to-end evaluation entangles a tokenizer's information retention with the reconstruction capacity of the downstream decoder, and the lack of a unified capacity contract across methods makes performance differences difficult to attribute. In this paper, we introduce Gaussian tokens to multi-modal brain tumor segmentation for the first time: each token carries not only a semantic feature but also a learned 3D center, anisotropic scale, and orientation, endowing the representation with explicit geometric support at negligible parameter cost. We further propose a frozen-token utility evaluation protocol: the trained tokenizer is frozen, its output is cast into a fixed-capacity serialized contract, and a shared lightweight Transformer probe independently measures each tokenizer's retained information under strictly matched conditions. Multi-seed paired statistical testing shows that GSToken consistently and substantially outperforms capacity-matched adaptive baselines under frozen probing, with uniform advantages across all tumor sub-regions, surface, and distance metrics. These results demonstrate that explicitly encoding spatial geometry within tokens significantly improves the information density of volumetric representations, offering a new design principle for compact 3D medical image representation and downstream reading.

</details>


### [37] [SemComp-Bench: Benchmarking Semantic Task Completion in Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17426)
*Keyu Tu,Zhuowei Chen,Mengqi Huang,Yuxin Wang,Jiahao Zhu,Zhendong Mao,Yongdong Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Semantic Task Completion Video Generation, an outcome-focused task requiring both task success and semantic grounding; creates SemComp-Data and SemComp-Bench for systematic evaluation; experiments show current models struggle to achieve outcomes with grounding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional video generation emphasizes appearance fidelity rather than task-specific outcomes and semantic grounding. There is a need for evaluation that measures whether generated videos accomplish the intended task while remaining semantically aligned with a reference image, independent of intermediate steps.

Method: Construct SemComp-Data: a six-domain evaluation dataset with each instance containing a reference image, a detailed instruction, a brief instruction, and an outcome-centric video clip; a four-stage curation pipeline to standardize data. Introduce SemComp-Bench: an evaluation protocol using a vision-language model to answer structured binary questions; reports OA (Outcome Achievement) and GR (Generation Reliability) scores.

Result: Experiments with representative video generation models show that achieving the intended outcomes while preserving task-relevant semantic grounding is challenging, indicating a gap between goal attainment and semantic alignment.

Conclusion: The framework enables systematic, outcome-centric evaluation of video generation and grounding, providing a benchmark and protocol that highlight the need for methods capable of jointly optimizing task success and semantic grounding.

Abstract: We introduce Semantic Task Completion Video Generation, an outcome-oriented video generation task. Under this formulation, success requires both achievement of the intended outcome and semantic grounding. Semantic grounding characterizes the correspondence between the reference image and the generated outcome in terms of high-level semantics relevant to the task. Evaluation focuses on the generated outcome and requires neither the presentation of a complete sequence of intermediate task steps nor conventional appearance consistency with the reference image. To support systematic evaluation, we construct SemComp-Data, an evaluation dataset covering six domains. Each instance comprises a reference image, a detailed instruction, a brief instruction, and an outcome-centric video clip. A scalable four-stage curation pipeline converts raw videos into standardized SemComp-Data instances. We further introduce SemComp-Bench, an evaluation protocol that uses a vision-language model (VLM) to answer structured binary questions. SemComp-Bench reports the OA Score and the GR Score for Outcome Achievement and Generation Reliability, respectively. Experiments on representative video generation models show that achieving intended outcomes while maintaining task-relevant semantic grounding in reference images remains challenging.

</details>


### [38] [Counterfactual Anatomy-guided Spatial-Temporal Decoding for Annotation-Free Hallucination Mitigation in Medical VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17427)
*Yifan Lu,Adinath Dukre,Abhijit Das,Ziyun Zou,Haolin Yang,Yutong Xie,Imran Razzak*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CAST is an inference-only framework that mitigates anatomical hallucinations in medical vision–language models by automatically identifying a compact, causally informative anatomical region via counterfactual occlusion, then applying a unified contrastive decoding (with classifier-free guidance and temporal contrast) to improve grounding. It requires no manual annotations and shows consistent gains on SLAKE and MIMIC-CXR across three Med-VLMs, beating baselines including ground-truth-reliant methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Med-VLMs hallucinate clinical details; decoding-time mitigations often lack anatomical awareness or rely on annotated data, limiting practicality. A fully inference-driven, anatomy-aware, annotation-free approach is needed.

Method: CAST discovers query-relevant anatomical regions via broad medical segmentation, selects a compact region using counterfactual occlusion that drops answer likelihood, and conducts a unified contrastive decoding guided by this region—combining classifier-free guidance to refine spatial attention and stepwise temporal contrast to regulate generation dynamics. No manual annotations are required.

Result: Empirical evaluation on SLAKE and MIMIC-CXR with three Med-VLMs shows CAST consistently outperforms strong baselines and decoding strategies that rely on ground truth; demonstrates improved spatial grounding and reduced hallucinations. Code available at the provided GitHub URL.

Conclusion: Automatically selecting compact anatomical regions yields effective, annotation-free guidance for reducing hallucinations and improving grounding in Med-VLMs, offering a practical and generalizable solution for inference-time mitigation.

Abstract: Medical vision-language models (Med-VLMs) have demonstrated strong performance on medical visual question answering, yet they remain prone to hallucination, generating clinically unsupported statements that are insufficiently grounded in image evidence. Mitigation methods applied during decoding offer a practical solution, but they typically lack anatomical awareness or rely heavily on ground truth annotations, which limits their applicability. We propose Counterfactual Anatomy-guided Spatial-Temporal decoding (CAST), a framework that operates entirely during inference and requires no manual annotations for anatomically grounded hallucination mitigation. CAST automatically discovers anatomical regions relevant to the given query through broad medical segmentation. It then selects a compact, causally informative area using counterfactual intervention based on the drop in answer likelihood under occlusion. Guided by this chosen region, CAST performs a unified contrastive decoding process, combining classifier-free guidance to correct spatial attention with stepwise temporal contrast to regulate generation dynamics. Experiments on the SLAKE and MIMIC-CXR datasets across three Med-VLMs demonstrate that CAST consistently outperforms strong baselines and surpasses decoding strategies reliant on ground truth. Our results indicate that compact, automatically selected regions provide highly effective contrastive guidance without expert annotations, offering a practical and generalizable solution for improving spatial grounding and reducing hallucinations. Code is available at https://github.com/csyifan/CAST.

</details>


### [39] [NGS-Marker: Robust Native Watermarking for 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17447)
*Hao Qin,Yukai Sun,Luyuan Chen,Mengxu Lu,Feng Zhang,Ming Kong,Zhenhong Du,Qiang Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces NGS-Marker, a native watermarking framework for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) that defends against partial infringement via a jointly trained injector/decoder and a gradient-based progressive injection strategy, with extensions to hybrid and multimodal watermarking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3DGS watermarking focuses on protecting rendered images (via pre-trained decoders) and fails to safeguard native 3D primitives, making them vulnerable to partial infringement where only a subset of Gaussians is reused.

Method: Jointly trained watermark injector and message decoder; gradient-based progressive injection to achieve full-scene coverage and enable ownership decoding from any local region; extensions include hybrid protection (native + indirect watermarks) and multimodal watermarking.

Result: Extensive experiments show that NGS-Marker robustly defends against partial infringement and offers practical flexibility for real-world deployment.

Conclusion: NGS-Marker provides effective native protection for 3D Gaussian Splatting, addressing partial infringement and enabling flexible deployment through hybrid and multimodal extensions.

Abstract: With the rapid development and adoption of 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), the need for effective copyright protection has become increasingly critical. Existing watermarking techniques for 3DGS mainly focus on protecting rendered images via pre-trained decoders, leaving the underlying 3D Gaussian primitives vulnerable to misuse. In particular, they are ineffective against Partial Infringement, where an adversary extracts and reuses only a subset of Gaussians. In this paper, we propose NGS-Marker, a novel native watermarking framework for 3DGS. It integrates a jointly trained watermark injector and message decoder, and employs a gradientbased progressive injection strategy to ensure full-scene coverage. This enables robust ownership decoding from any local region. We further extend NGS-Marker with hybrid protection (combining native and indirect watermarks) and support for multimodal watermarking. Extensive experiments demonstrate that NGS-Marker effectively defends against partial infringement while offering practical flexibility for real-world deployment.

</details>


### [40] [S$^3$AM: A Single-Stream SAM with Reliability-Calibrated Frequency Adapter for Multi-modal Salient Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17475)
*Ruichao Hou,Boyue Xu,Tongwei Ren,Dongming Zhou,Gangshan Wu,Jinde Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Single-stream SAM-based MSOD with reliability-calibrated frequency adaptation and mixture of frequency experts achieves competitive MSOD with 12.2M trainable params across RGB-D/T/NIR, reducing backbone duplication.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce computational cost and avoid noisy propagation in dual-stream SAM-adapted MSOD by enabling a single-stream architecture with controlled frequency injection.

Method: Introduce a mixture of frequency experts using stationary wavelet transform for cross-modal frequency fusion; implement a reliability-calibrated frequency adapter with dual-gate mechanism to propagate calibrated residuals across transformer stages; use a hypernetwork-guided semantic-structural decoder with Mamba-based detail recovery.

Result: On RGB-D, RGB-T, RGB-NIR SOD benchmarks, achieves competitive performance with only 12.20M trainable parameters (5.4% of total). The code will be available.

Conclusion: The framework delivers parameter-efficient, reliable MSOD with explicit control over auxiliary frequency cues, offering a practical single-stream alternative to SAM-based MSOD.

Abstract: Vision foundation models have recently advanced multi-modal salient object detection (MSOD) through parameter-efficient tuning and prompt learning. However, existing Segment Anything Model (SAM)-adapted MSOD methods often rely on dual-stream encoders or auxiliary prompt generators, leading to redundant computation. Although a single-stream alternative can reduce this cost, early fusion may also propagate noisy or misaligned auxiliary high-frequency cues through the backbone. In this paper, we propose a novel single-stream framework that integrates reliability-calibrated frequency adaptation into the adopted SAM backbone for MSOD. It avoids duplicated foundation backbones while explicitly controlling auxiliary frequency injection. Specifically, we design a mixture of frequency experts module, which uses the stationary wavelet transform to decompose each modality and aggregate cross-modal frequency information. We further introduce a reliability-calibrated frequency adapter with a dual-gate calibration mechanism, which selectively propagates the calibrated residual across transformer stages while jointly controlling its injection strength and cross-modal reliability. A hypernetwork-guided semantic-structural decoder then combines semantic mask features from the adopted backbone with Mamba-based structural detail recovery. Comprehensive experiments on RGB-D, RGB-T, and RGB-NIR salient object detection benchmarks validate that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance with only 12.20M trainable parameters, accounting for 5.4\% of the total parameters. The code will be available at https://github.com/xuboyue1999/SSSAM.

</details>


### [41] [NeuroPath: Brain-Inspired Dual-Pathway Graph Convolutional Networks for Skeleton-Based Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17487)
*Kanglei Zhou,Ruizhi Cai,Hubert P. H. Shum,Frederick W. B. Li,Xiaohui Liang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Dual-Pathway Graph Convolutional Network (NeuroPath) for skeleton-based action recognition with separate spatial and temporal pathways, a group graph convolution block to model key parts, and inter-pathway dynamic fusion, achieving improved results on Kinetics Skeleton 400, NTU RGB+D 60, and NTU RGB+D 120.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Implicit coupling of spatial and temporal information in existing STGCN models leads to uneven exploitation of skeletal cues across modalities. Decoupling into specialized pathways aims to better leverage complementary spatial structure and motion dynamics.

Method: Propose a dual-pathway architecture (NeuroPath) with transformation units creating pathway-specific skeletal representations. Introduce a group graph convolution block to identify key body parts and model their spatial-temporal dependencies. Implement inter-pathway dynamic fusion modules to integrate complementary information across the two pathways.

Result: Consistent performance improvements are reported on standard benchmarks (Kinetics Skeleton 400, NTU RGB+D 60, NTU RGB+D 120), validating the effectiveness of dual-pathway spatial-temporal modeling for skeleton-based action recognition.

Conclusion: Dual-pathway modeling effectively leverages complementary spatial and temporal cues in skeleton data, with dynamic fusion enhancing inter-modal collaboration and overall recognition accuracy.

Abstract: Skeleton-based action recognition aims to recognize human actions from sequences of human joint coordinates. Most existing Spatial-Temporal Graph Convolutional Networks (STGCNs) have achieved promising results by modeling skeletal structures with implicit spatial-temporal representations. However, our empirical study reveals a clear performance imbalance across different skeletal modalities, indicating that implicitly coupling spatial and temporal information limits the full exploitation of complementary structural and motion cues. Inspired by the ventral and dorsal pathways in human perception, we propose Dual-Pathway Graph Convolutional Networks (NeuroPath), which adopt a dual-pathway architecture for separate yet collaborative modeling of spatial and temporal information. Specifically, transformation units first convert the input into pathway-specific skeletal representations, allowing each pathway to focus on complementary aspects of human motion. To further capture coordinated joint behaviors and their interrelationships, we introduce a group graph convolution block that dynamically identifies key body parts and models their spatial-temporal dependencies. In addition, inter-pathway dynamic fusion modules integrate complementary inter-modal information across pathways, facilitating higher-level semantic interpretation of actions. Extensive experiments on Kinetics Skeleton 400, NTU RGB+D 60, and NTU RGB+D 120 demonstrate consistent performance improvements, validating the effectiveness of dual-pathway spatial-temporal modeling for skeleton-based action recognition.

</details>


### [42] [When More Foundation Models Means Less: Diagnosing and Addressing Multi-View Fusion Failure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17490)
*Yibo Liu,Bowen Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KAGES is a label-aware, kernel-alignment-based greedy method for selecting a compact, task-aligned subset of encoders from a large pool to fuse, improving performance vs full fusion and other selection methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Downstream performance with many encoders is non-monotonic due to redundancy and misalignment; indiscriminate fusion can saturate or hurt accuracy. A principled view-set composition is needed.

Method: Order frozen encoders by marginal gain in centred kernel-target alignment; greedily select according to a label-aware objective without training a downstream classifier; time O(n^2) per evaluation; offers a conditional prefix guarantee when monotonicity and positive submodularity ratio hold.

Result: Across five recognition regimes and data protocols, KAGES improves average AULC compared to full fusion (3.3–5.8 points) and outperforms DPP and facility-location baselines; image retrieval shows task-dependent saturation; frozen-LLM fusion shows peak-then-decline behavior.

Conclusion: Effective large-pool fusion requires selecting a compact, task-aligned subset of views rather than fusing more encoders; KAGES provides a practical, theoretically grounded approach with favorable empirical results.

Abstract: Foundation-model hubs turn multi-view fusion into a selection problem: from a large heterogeneous encoder pool, which views should be fused, and how many? We show that downstream performance is non-monotonic in the number of fused encoders; later views can be redundant or task-misaligned, causing accuracy to saturate or decline. We formalise this setting as view-set composition and propose KAGES (Kernel-Alignment Greedy Encoder Selector), a label-aware method that orders frozen encoders by their marginal gain in centred kernel-target alignment. KAGES requires no downstream classifier training during selection, evaluates each candidate in $\mathcal{O}(n^2)$ time independent of encoder dimension, and admits a conditional $(1-e^{-γ})$ prefix-wise guarantee under monotonicity and a positive submodularity ratio. Across five recognition regimes and low-shot, larger-pool, and full-data protocols, KAGES improves average AULC over full fusion by 3.9, 5.8, and 3.3 points, respectively, and exceeds DPP and facility-location selection in average AULC. Image retrieval exhibits later, task-dependent saturation along the KAGES ordering, while peak-then-decline reproduces in frozen-LLM fusion. These results show that effective large-pool fusion depends on selecting a compact, task-aligned set of views rather than indiscriminately fusing more encoders.

</details>


### [43] [Training with synthetic data for drone detection in thermal imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17799)
*Tanel Liiv,Sander Soodla,Nzamba Bignoumba,Alma M. Liezenga,Toomas Pruuden*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Synthetic-first training with real-data finetuning improves MWIR/LWIR G2A drone detection; dataset alignment and semantic feature-space alignment are key.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MWIR/LWIR drone detection is hampered by low texture, sensor noise, weak contrast, and scarce labels; seek a practical training strategy leveraging synthetic data to bootstrap learning and reduce domain gap.

Method: A synthetic scene generation pipeline is used to pretrain detection models, followed by fine-tuning on real IR data. The study analyzes domain gaps, compares dataset alignment versus model scale, and examines radiometric properties and semantic alignment.

Result: Synthetic data provides a strong initialization for object representations; real in-domain IR data is essential for deployment; even small amounts of real data significantly reduce domain gaps; dataset alignment has a stronger impact than model scale; semantic alignment in feature space is the strongest predictor of performance; radiometric properties also contribute to robustness.

Conclusion: This work lays a foundation for effectively combining synthetic and real IR data for G2A drone detection, emphasizing dataset alignment and semantic-space alignment as critical factors.

Abstract: Ground-to-Air (G2A) drone detection in medium- and long-wave infrared (MWIR/LWIR) imagery is challenging due to reduced texture information, sensor noise, weak thermal contrast, and the scarcity of annotated data. This work investigates a synthetic-first training strategy that combines synthetic scene generation with fine-tuning on real data. We show that synthetic data provides an effective basis for learning initial object representations, while real in-domain thermal imagery is still essential for reliable deployment. Even small amounts of real IR data substantially reduce domain gaps. Our experiments indicate that dataset alignment has a stronger impact on performance than model scale. Finally, our analysis of the dataset suggests that semantic alignment in feature space is the strongest predictor of model performance, while radiometric properties such as entropy and dynamic range also contribute to detection robustness. This work provides a foundation for combining synthetic and real IR data for effective G2A drone detection.

</details>


### [44] [SE-MoLoRA: Shared-Expert LoRA Adapters for Domain-Specific Photographic Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17514)
*Bishwash Khanal,Anlan Zhang,Sasu Tarkoma,Tommi Mikkonen,Abhishek Kumar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SE-MoLoRA is a modular, parameter-efficient adaptation framework for domain-specific photographic critique that decouples general photographic knowledge from specialist residual judgments via a shared LoRA expert and routed adapters, enabling targeted critique with fewer parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the entanglement of semantic content and aesthetic judgment in vision-language models by enabling controllable, domain-specific photographic critique without training full separate models.

Method: A shared rank-64 LoRA expert remains always active, with rank-32 routed adapters for composition, lighting, and technical quality. A lightweight query router selects relevant specialist. Orthogonal regularization encourages disentangled representations. Training data is distilled from the Reddit Photo Critique Dataset into domain-labeled critiques.

Result: On held-out critique generation, SE-MoLoRA raises BERTScore-F1 from 0.2317 to 0.4215 versus monolithic LoRA, is preferred in 84.6% of pairwise comparisons, and uses fewer active parameters than separate specialist models. An SVD-based ablation indicates shared-specialist decomposition and orthogonal regularization reduce expert overlap.

Conclusion: Modular adaptation enhances controllability and specificity in multimodal photographic critique, enabling targeted, domain-focused evaluation with improved performance and parameter efficiency.

Abstract: Vision-language models can describe images fluently, but they often fail to provide actionable photographic critique because semantic content and aesthetic judgment remain entangled. We propose SE-MoLoRA, a modular parameter-efficient adaptation framework for domain-specific photographic assessment. The method separates general photographic knowledge from specialist residual judgments using an always-active shared LoRA expert and routed adapters for composition, lighting, and technical quality. A lightweight query router selects the relevant specialist, enabling targeted critique without training separate full models. A rank-64 shared adapter captures broad photographic vocabulary, while rank-32 specialists learn domain-specific residuals with an orthogonal regularization penalty that encourages disentangled representations. Training data is obtained by distilling the Reddit Photo Critique Dataset into domain-labeled critique samples. On held-out critique generation, SE-MoLoRA improves BERTScore-F1 from 0.2317 to 0.4215 over monolithic LoRA and is preferred in 84.6\% of pairwise comparisons, while using fewer active parameters than separate specialist models. SVD-based ablation study shows that shared-specialist decomposition and orthogonal regularization reduce expert overlap. These results demonstrate that modular adaptation improves controllability and specificity in multimodal photographic critique.

</details>


### [45] [Looking Beyond the Scale: Do Surgical Skill Models Learn Transferable Representations Across Assessment Rubrics?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17519)
*Hanna Hoffmann,Felix von Bechtolsheim,Stefanie Speidel,Rebecca Hisey*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Cross-domain transfer of vision-based surgical skill representations is asymmetrical: JIGSAWS-trained models transfer to LASANA with CCC ~0.77–0.80, but LASANA-trained models fail to transfer to JIGSAWS; results imply dataset-specific annotation issues and that the visual component dominates skill prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Assess whether learned representations of surgical proficiency transfer across scoring systems (GOALS/OSATS) between LASANA and JIGSAWS; probe end-to-end transfer, optimization landscape via ASAM, and domain-invariant pretraining via self-supervised/contrastive methods.

Method: Systematic cross-domain evaluation with three diagnostic strategies: (1) end-to-end supervised transfer; (2) Adaptive Sharpness-Aware Minimization to test loss landscape and generalization; (3) augmentation-based self-supervised/contrastive pretraining for domain-invariant features; cross-domain transfer in both directions with a disjoint-participant, held-out JIGSAWS test set.

Result: Asymmetric transfer observed: JIGSAWS-pretrained backbones achieve CCC 0.77–0.80 on LASANA, approaching end-to-end baseline, indicating feasible cross-rubric transfer under consistent supervision; LASANA→JIGSAWS transfer fails across methods, likely due to annotation inconsistencies; Kinetics-pretrained backbones suggest the task-specific heads carry most of the skill-prediction burden while the backbone mostly provides spatiotemporal features.

Conclusion: Visual cues dominate skill prediction but transferable skill features are not robustly disentangled; cross-domain transfer is possible when supervision aligns, yet annotation inconsistencies impede reverse transfer; further work is needed to disentangle transferable skill representations from domain-specific visual context.

Abstract: Vision-based surgical skill assessment has shown strong in-domain results, yet a fundamental question remains unasked: do these models learn transferable representations of surgical proficiency, or do they merely encode dataset-specific visual patterns?
  This paper systematically analyzes what limits cross-domain skill transfer between the GOALS and OSATS assessment scales using the LASANA and JIGSAWS datasets. Each evaluated method serves a targeted diagnostic purpose: end-to-end training to test whether supervised skill learning transfers directly, Adaptive Sharpness-Aware Minimization (ASAM) to probe whether flatter loss landscapes improve generalization, and augmentation-based self-supervised and contrastive learning to assess whether domain-invariant pretraining decouples skill from visual context. Transfer is evaluated in both directions using a disjoint-participant held-out test set for JIGSAWS.
  Results reveal an asymmetry: backbones pretrained on JIGSAWS achieve CCC values of 0.77 to 0.80 on LASANA, closely matching the end-to-end baseline, showing cross-rubric transfer is feasible when the target domain provides consistent supervision. Transfer to JIGSAWS fails across all methods, likely due to annotation inconsistencies. Control experiments with a Kinetics-pretrained backbone suggest task-specific heads carry the majority of the skill prediction burden, while the backbone need only provide adequate spatiotemporal features.
  These findings offer a new perspective on vision-based skill assessment: the central question of whether skill representations transfer across scoring systems has not been previously investigated. Results indicate the visual component is dominant but not solely responsible for skill prediction; further work is needed to conclusively disentangle transferable skill features from those bound to a specific visual domain.

</details>


### [46] [BrainNorm: A Foundation Model that knows Normal via Semantic Atlas Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17521)
*Madhumitha Venkatesh,Shanawaj S Madarkar,Konda Reddy Mopuri*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BrainNorm is a normative foundation model for MRI that learns a semantic atlas latent space (SAL) of atlas-parcel embeddings from ~66k T1-weighted scans, enabling age-consistent template trajectories, localized deviation scoring, and zero-shot/few-shot disease prediction with strong linear-probe performance and alignment with neurodegeneration pathology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the need for normative, interpretable MRI representations that capture healthy aging and support data-efficient, generalizable disease prediction across cohorts.

Method: Language-image style contrastive pretraining on healthy cohorts across ages to learn the Semantic Atlas Latent space (SAL). Each scan is represented as atlas-parcel embeddings; healthy aging template trajectories are derived to support age-consistent template matching and localized deviation scoring relative to chronological age. Evaluation spans 6 downstream cohorts and 25 task-settings (age estimation, brain-age gap, parcel identification, single- and multi-disease classification) under direct inference, zero-shot, few-shot, and full-data linear-probe regimes. Fine-tuning is performed on healthy-only data for downstream tasks.

Result: BrainNorm generalizes across 6 cohorts and 25 task-settings. It enables zero-shot disease prediction via parcel-wise abnormalities and improves performance with fine-tuning on healthy data. Across all classification tasks, linear probing on BrainNorm's frozen embeddings outperforms 9 baselines finetuned end-to-end. Localized SAL-deviations align with established neurodegeneration pathology in clinical literature.

Conclusion: SAL-based healthy-aging templates and localized deviations offer robust, interpretable biomarkers for aging and disease, with strong generalization, efficient downstream linear probing, and alignment to neuropathology.

Abstract: We introduce BrainNorm, a normative foundation model, trained and tested on ~66,000 T1-weighted structural MRI (T1w sMRI) scans. By leveraging language-image style contrastive pretraining on healthy cohorts across ages, BrainNorm learns a Semantic Atlas Latent space (SAL), where each scan is represented as a set of atlas-parcel embeddings. This yields parcel-specific healthy aging template trajectories that support age-consistent template matching and localized deviation scoring relative to a subject's chronological age. Across 6 downstream cohorts, BrainNorm demonstrates generalization evaluated across 25 task-setting combinations spanning age estimation, brain-age gap estimation, parcel identification, and single- & multi-disease classification tasks under direct inference, zero-shot, few-shot & full-data linear-probe settings. The resulting deviation patterns in SAL space enable zero-shot tasks for disease prediction using parcel-wise abnormalities. Fine-tuning on healthy-only cohorts of downstream datasets further improves the performance of various tasks. Across all classification tasks, linear probing on BrainNorm's frozen embeddings outperforms 9 baselines finetuned under end-to-end supervision. Furthermore, the localized deviations identified by BrainNorm across various neurodegenerative disorders closely align with established neurodegeneration pathology in clinical literature.

</details>


### [47] [Explainable AI-Powered Framework for Video-Based Skill Assessment in Cataract Surgery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17522)
*Mohammad Javad Ahmadi,Hamid D. Taghirad*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Explainable AI for automated cataract-surgery skill assessment using a large video dataset; strong alignment with expert CSAS ratings (up to 87% accuracy) and objective motion-based metrics that may augment/redefine training evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address persistent surgical workforce shortages and limitations of traditional training by developing automated, data-driven, explainable skill assessment tools to complement subjective scoring.

Method: An AI-powered framework leveraging computer vision and signal processing to extract 10 objective motion-based metrics from cataract-surgery videos. Uses what is claimed as the world's largest cataract-video dataset (2,000 recordings). Experimental validation on 83 videos with expert subjective ratings via a new Capsulorhexis Skill Assessment System (CSAS). Emphasizes explainable outputs rather than opaque classifications.

Result: Automatic metrics exhibit strong correlations with expert subjective evaluations; up to 87% accuracy in skill assessment. Individual metrics show robust alignment with subjective CSAS ratings, supporting the framework’s capacity to model surgical expertise.

Conclusion: The framework offers explainable, objective indicators that can complement or potentially replace subjective scoring, supported by a large dataset and demonstrated correlation with expert assessments, with implications for scalable, data-driven surgical education.

Abstract: Persistent shortages in the surgical workforce and inherent limitations of traditional training methods highlight the necessity of automated, data-driven approaches in surgical education. This study addresses these challenges by introducing a novel, explainable AI-powered framework for automated skill assessment, specifically focusing on cataract surgery. We present the world's largest dataset of cataract surgery videos, comprising 2,000 recordings. Additionally, we propose an AI-powered analytical framework that employs advanced computer vision and signal-processing techniques to automatically evaluate surgical videos to derive objective, quantitative performance indicators that complement or potentially replace subjective scoring methods. A significant advantage of our framework over previous methods lies precisely in its explainability of outputs, elevating it beyond merely an opaque skill classification tool. Through experimental analysis of 83 cataract surgery videos, we demonstrate that the automatically computed metrics exhibit strong correlations with expert-based subjective evaluations, achieving up to 87% accuracy in surgical skill assessment. Each metric was individually examined, and expert surgeons provided subjective ratings using the newly introduced Capsulorhexis Skill Assessment System (CSAS). These subjective assessments were compared with ten objective motion-based metrics extracted through our framework. The results indicated a robust correlation between subjective ratings and automated indicators, underscoring the framework's capacity to accurately model surgical expertise.

</details>


### [48] [GroupForward: Building Referable 3D Scenes via Instance-Grouped Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17535)
*Qijian Tian,Zimeng Wu,Xuhong Wang,Lizhuang Ma,Xin Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces GroupForward, an instance-grouped 3D Gaussian Splatting framework, and RSRF, a referential scene reasoning pipeline that leverages instance-level grouping and a vision-language model to perform referential segmentation from sparse, unposed multi-view data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addresses the need for simultaneous 3D reconstruction and semantic understanding for embodied agents.
